⏺ I checked both models' readings for all 8 receipts and zoomed into the images.
  Your instincts are mostly right. Some flags are real misreads, but most of
  the noise comes from our own rules and UI, not from the receipts.

  Per receipt

  #: 12
  File: 442334
  What actually happened: Date: Haiku read "28 Mar", Flash read "26 Mar". The
  image says 26. Subtotal: Haiku took "Total Amount: $8.20" as the subtotal;
  Flash said no subtotal is printed. It equals the total, so it's the same
  number.
  Verdict: Date flag is real (one model misread it). Subtotal flag is noise.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 13
  File: 442343
  What actually happened: Haiku read 2016, Flash read 2018. The image says 2018.
  Verdict: Real, and the system is doing its job here.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 14
  File: 442375
  What actually happened: Haiku misread 8.50 as 6.50 on both total lines. Flash
  read 8.50. Both models read the line items correctly (5.50 + 3.00).
  Verdict: Bug: the line-items check compares against Haiku's wrong total. The
  quote shows Haiku's line, which is why "6.50" looked printed. The items sum
  (8.50) actually tells us which total is right.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 15
  File: 469671
  What actually happened: Same "Total Amount = total" subtotal issue. "Currency
  inferred" is only a note and doesn't lower confidence. "tax: check" is a UI 
  bug: null always renders as "check", even when both models agree no tax is
  printed.
  Verdict: Noise
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 16
  File: 230616
  What actually happened: Currency: no symbol is printed. Haiku guessed MYR,
  Flash left it null, and we count that as a disagreement. Rounding: Haiku
  returned 0 with no printed line.
  Verdict: Both are noise. A missing 0.00 shouldn't be checked, as you said.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 17
  File: 230621
  What actually happened: Haiku read the unit price 1.887 as the line amount;
  Flash read 2.00. Flash's items add up to the subtotal both models agreed on
  (7.30), and Haiku's don't. Line items are only written out when both readers
  agree, so none appear.
  Verdict: We could settle this with arithmetic.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 18
  File: 433543
  What actually happened: Haiku read "SDN EHD". Yes, a vendor disagreement makes

  it MEDIUM by rule. "MYR or SGD" is because $ isn't in our currency map.
  Verdict: The typo flag is real. The currency flag is noise.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 19
  File: 442366
  What actually happened: Haiku counted the "Discount 0.30" twice (as a line
  item
  and as an adjustment). Flash's items add up exactly to the total (22.60).
  "tax: check" is the same UI bug as #15.
  Verdict: We could settle this with arithmetic.

  Across all 44 receipts, "subtotal computed (not printed)" appears on 23 and 
  "currency inferred" on 9. Neither changes the status, but both clutter every
  card.

  Proposed fixes, in order of impact

  1. Settle line items with arithmetic. When the readers disagree on items but
     only one reader's items add up to a subtotal or total that both agreed on,
     use that reader's items and add a note. This fixes #17 and #19 and several
     others (230621, 442366, 442388, 568855). Only run the items-vs-total check
     when the total agrees (fixes the #14 bug).
  2. Show evidence for the total when it disagrees: "check total: 6.50 or 8.50,
     line items (both read) add to 8.50". Show each reader's quote next to its
     own value.
  3. Stop counting these as disagreements:
     - a subtotal from one reader vs "absent" from the other, when the value
       equals the total
     - a currency of null vs X when no symbol is printed
     - a rounding of 0 with no printed line
     - "$" treated as ambiguous rather than inferring SGD
  4. Remove the two constant notes ("subtotal computed", "currency inferred")
     from the review page, and keep them in the CSV only. Mark computed
     subtotals with a small "computed" tag.
  5. UI: show "—" for values both readers agree aren't printed, and "check" only
     for real disagreements.
  6. Optional: a third read on key-field disagreements (majority of 3). This is
     the "targeted re-ask" idea already in PLAN.md's later list. It would
     resolve #12, #13, #14, #18 and the TED/TEO vendor. Flash was right in all
     of those, but a third read from a different model is a fairer tie-breaker
     than always trusting Flash. It costs roughly one extra call on about 10 of
     the 44 receipts.

  Per-item confidence?

  I wouldn't ask the model for a confidence score. Self-reported confidence is
  poorly calibrated, and our notes already say legible: true tells us nothing.
  I'd get the same thing from agreement between the readers, per line:
  - Mark each line item as agreed or disputed.
  - Point the reason at the specific line: check "PPD 4MM DENLIME": 1.89 or 
    2.00, not totals read as 7.19 or 7.30.

  That's more useful to a reviewer, and it fits with fix 1.

  My guess is that fixes 1–5 move most of the 21 MEDIUMs to HIGH without hiding
  any real misreads. Want me to implement 1–5, with tests, and re-run? Fix 6 is
  a separate decision because it adds cost.

  ⎿  Read S4.pdf (89.8KB)

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The basic change: stop treating any disagreement between the two readers as 
  doubt. Let the receipt's own arithmetic decide, and use agreement as a second 
  signal.

  Why most flags are noise now

  Our rule is "fill a value only when both readers agree." It fails in two ways:
  - Different wording, same meaning gets flagged: a subtotal that equals the
    total vs "absent", rounding 0 vs null, a currency guess vs null.
  - A tie arithmetic can settle gets flagged: in #14, #17 and #19, one reading
    adds up and the other doesn't. We ignore that.

  Three changes

  1. Normalize each reading before comparing. If the subtotal equals the total,
     set it to null. Set a rounding of 0 to null. Set the currency to null when
     no symbol is printed. This removes definition noise (#12, #14, #15, #16,
     #18).
  2. Arithmetic picks the reading. Check each reading on its own: do the items
     add up to the subtotal or total, and does subtotal + tax come to the total?
     If exactly one reading passes, use it. This fixes #14 (8.50), #17 and #19
     (line items now shown).
  3. Re-read once when the readers still disagree (the article's
     evaluator-optimizer pattern). Ask a model again, but only about the
     disputed field. For example: "date read as 26 or 28 Mar, read that line
     again". It stops after one round. This fixes #12, #13 and #18, at about 10
     extra calls per run.

  The new confidence levels:
  - HIGH: the readers agree and the sums check.
  - MEDIUM: settled by arithmetic or by the re-read. Show it with the reason.
  - LOW: still unresolved, or the sums fail.

  Fit with the brief

  - Validation logic: the checks now choose values instead of only adding notes,
    so they're no longer cosmetic.
  - Calibration: LOW means "a human must decide", not "the models phrased it
    differently".
  - Output usability: fewer, specific reasons, like check "PPD 4MM DENLIME": 
    1.89 or 2.00.

  Fit with the article

  - Workflow, not an agent: the steps are fixed, and the model is used only
    where it is needed.
  - Ground truth from the environment: arithmetic is that ground truth.
  - Evaluator-optimizer: only where the evaluation criteria are clear, with a
    hard stop after one round.
  - Keep it simple: no confidence score asked from the model.

  Scope: changes 1 and 2 are code only, about 1 hour, no extra API cost. Change
  3 needs new API calls and adds about 30 minutes. I'd do 1 and 2, re-run the
  eval, then decide on 3.

  Should I go ahead?

✻ Baked for 25s · done 3:12 PM

❯ I am not sure on 2nd change                                                   
  wouldnt it bring back a bug where haiku's invented items adds up and flash's  
  honest nulls didn't?                                                          

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Yes, it would, as I wrote it. Consistent made-up numbers pass the sum checks;
  PLAN.md already lists that as a known limit.

  Change 2 is only safe with two guards:

  1. Number vs number only. Arithmetic may choose between two different numbers.
     It never chooses a number over a null or an illegible mark. Your bug was
     Haiku's numbers against Flash's nulls, so it stays flagged.
  2. Check against a shared value. The winning reading must add up to a value
     both readers agreed on, not just its own total. An invented set of items
     that matches its own reader's total doesn't count.

  Against the cases:

  ┌──────────────────────┬────────────────────┬────────────────┬────────────┐
  │         Case         │      Guard 1       │    Guard 2     │   Result   │
  ├──────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────┼────────────┤
  │ Old bug (Haiku       │                    │                │ still      │
  │ invents amounts,     │ number vs null     │ —              │ flagged    │
  │ Flash has nulls)     │                    │                │            │
  ├──────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────┼────────────┤
  │ #14 total 6.50 vs    │ number vs number   │ both read the  │ 8.50 wins  │
  │ 8.50                 │                    │ items as 8.50  │            │
  ├──────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────┼────────────┤
  │ #17 item 1.887 vs    │                    │ both read the  │ Flash's    │
  │ 2.00                 │ number vs number   │ subtotal as    │ items win  │
  │                      │                    │ 7.30           │            │
  ├──────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────┼────────────┤
  │ #19 Haiku's extra    │ the winner (Flash) │ both read the  │ Flash's    │
  │ −0.30 line           │  has fewer values, │ total as 22.60 │ items win  │
  │                      │  not more          │                │            │
  └──────────────────────┴────────────────────┴────────────────┴────────────┘

  The winner is always the more conservative reading, so this never fills a
  value one reader left empty. Settled rows show as MEDIUM with the reason, not
  HIGH.

  Should I build 1 and 2 with these guards and add a test for the old bug?

⏺ I built both changes with the two guards. The results changed as expected, and
  the eval still shows 0 wrong values in any status.

  What changed
  - src/normalize.ts (new): before comparing, a subtotal equal to the total
    becomes null, a 0 rounding becomes null, and 0 adjustments are dropped. A
    currency with no printed symbol is no longer flagged when one reader leaves
    it empty.
  - src/score.ts: arithmetic picks between two different numbers only. The
    winner must match a value both readers agreed on, and it can't have more
    amounts than the loser. The line-items check now runs against the chosen
    total, which fixes the false alarm in #14.
  - Row.itemsAgreed is replaced by lineItems, so line_items.csv uses the settled
    items.

  The arithmetic picks, checked by hand:
  - #14: total 8.50 is correct.
  - #17: item 2.00 is correct.
  - #19: Flash's items without the duplicated discount are correct.
  - 442388: I checked the image. Haiku made up an extra 1.00 line, and the guard
    kept Flash's reading (21.60), which is correct.
  - 568855, 433556 and 268275 are plausible from the readings.

  Tests: all 76 pass, including the old bug (made-up amounts vs honest nulls
  stay unresolved in both reader orders) and "a total one reader left empty is
  never filled".

  Still open (not in this change):
  - Dates in #12 and #13, and the vendor typo in #18, are real misreads. The
    re-read step (change 3) would settle them.
  - "$" is still read as MYR or SGD in #18.
  - The review page still shows "check" for tax on receipts with no tax printed.
  - The notes "subtotal computed" and "currency inferred" still show on every
    card.

  Nothing is committed. Want me to commit, then fix the UI and the notes?

  ⎿  [Image #21]

⏺ The cause: .card{display:flex} overrides the browser's default
  [hidden]{display:none}, so setting hidden on a card does nothing. That's a
  one-line CSS fix.

⏺ The filter is fixed. The cards had display:flex, which overrode the hidden
  attribute, so no card ever hid. I added .card[hidden]{display:none} and a test
  for it, then regenerated out/review.html. Tests pass. I haven't clicked it in
  a browser, so reload the page to confirm.

  Other review UI changes, most useful first:

  1. "not printed" vs "check". A field both readers agree isn't printed (e.g.
     tax on a no-tax receipt) should show "—", not "check". Only real doubt
     should get the colour.
  2. Show both readings inline, e.g. total 8.50 (read 6.50 / 8.50). The reviewer
     sees the choice next to the value, without matching it to a bullet.
  3. Turn the constant notes into tags. "subtotal computed" and "currency
     inferred" would become a small grey tag next to the value, not a bullet on
     23 cards.
  4. Default to "Needs review". Open with a UNREADABLE + LOW + MEDIUM filter
     instead of All, which is what the brief asks for.
  5. Mark disputed line items. Highlight the one line the readers disagreed on,
     e.g. 1.887 / 2.00, instead of only showing the totals.
  6. Bigger image on click. Show it in place, not in a new tab.

  Skip for now: editing values and saving the corrections. It needs saved state
  and an export step, which is too big for the 2-hour scope; it's a good "with
  more time" item for NOTES.md.

  I'd do 1–4 (small, and they come straight from your earlier feedback). Should
  I go ahead?

※ recap: Goal: cut false flags in the receipt agent so confidence matches what the receipts really show; scoring and the review page changes are done, tested and checked in a browser. Next: your go-ahead to commit them as two commits. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ We have three confidence levels: low, medium, and high. It seems that we have 
  paired low confidence with some keys and medium with some keys. In both of    
  them, the action is that you need to check them. There doesn't seem to be a   
  significant difference between both of them. Do we even need these categories 
  then?                                                                         

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ You're right that LOW and MEDIUM both say "check". The split is by which field
  is in doubt (total/date → LOW, the rest → MEDIUM), and that doesn't tell the
  reviewer what to do.

  The current output shows the problem. Of the 16 MEDIUM rows:
  - 11 have every value filled. A choice was made (e.g. total 8.50 settled by
    arithmetic), or only the line items are in doubt. The reviewer just
    confirms.
  - 5 have an empty value (vendor, tax or subtotal). The reviewer has to read
    the image and type it in. That's the same work as the 2 LOW rows, where the
    date is empty.

  So there is a real difference in effort. We're just splitting on the wrong
  thing.

  My recommendation: keep three levels, but define them by the action needed.

  ┌────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────┐
  │ Level  │                    Meaning                     │   Rows now    │
  ├────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ LOW    │ Fill in: a header value is empty because the   │ 7 (2 LOW + 5  │
  │        │ readings disagree, or the sums fail            │ MEDIUM)       │
  ├────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ MEDIUM │ Confirm: all values are filled, but a choice   │ 11            │
  │        │ was made or the line items are unsure          │               │
  ├────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ HIGH   │ Nothing to do                                  │ 26            │
  └────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────┘

  Why not collapse to two levels (OK / REVIEW):
  - The brief asks for a confidence score you can sort by, and "fix these 7
    first, then glance at 11" is a better review order than 18 rows in one pile.
  - For calibration, each level makes a claim the eval can check: LOW means "a
    value is missing", and MEDIUM means "the values are probably right". So far,
    0 filled values in MEDIUM are wrong.

  The change itself is small: the level comes from "is any header value empty or
  failing?" instead of a list of key fields. I'd also rename the page labels to
  "Fill in" and "Confirm" so the action is obvious.

  Should I make the change?

✻ Worked for 25s · done 3:44 PM

❯ what do u mean choice was made                                                

⏺ "A choice was made" means the two readers gave different numbers, and our code
  picked one of them by checking the receipt's own arithmetic.

  Example, #14 (X51005442375):
  - Haiku read the total as 6.50 and Flash read 8.50.
  - Both read the line items as 5.50 + 3.00 = 8.50.
  - So the code filled in total = 8.50 and showed (read 6.50 / 8.50).

  The value is filled and probably right. But it came from a rule, not from both
  readers agreeing, so a person should glance at the image and confirm it.

  That applies to 7 of the 11 rows. I lumped the other 4 in too loosely; they
  are line-item problems where nothing was picked:
  - X51005337867, X51005447844: some item amounts couldn't be read (faded or cut
    off).
  - X51005365187: the readers' item lists disagree, and arithmetic couldn't
    settle it.
  - X51005444046: both readers agree on the items, but they add up to 66.17
    while the total is 66.15.

  In those 4, all the header values (vendor, date, total…) are filled, but the
  line items are missing or doubtful. For X51005365187 and X51005447844 no line
  items are written at all, so the reviewer would have to type them in.

  So the cleaner split is:
  - Fill in: any value is empty, including missing line items → 7 + 2 = 9 rows.
  - Confirm: everything is filled, but something was settled by a rule or
    doesn't quite add up → 9 rows.

  Should I build it that way?

❯ Are u sure, if agreement is there and  arithmetic is also passed, it should   
  not be flagged                                                                
  and also I checked in one case there is a bug, were item sum to 66.17,        
  rounding is -0.02 the items check should include rounding and it doesnt

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Yes. If both readers agree and the arithmetic passes, the row should be HIGH.
  Your X51005444046 case breaks that rule because of the bug you found.

  The bug: the line-items check compares the items sum straight to the total,
  ignoring rounding and adjustments. X51005444046: the items add up to 66.17,
  and 66.17 − 0.02 rounding = 66.15, which is the total. Both readers agree on
  all of it, so it should be HIGH. The same mistake is in two other places:
  - the computed subtotal (it uses total − tax, and should also take out
    rounding and adjustments)
  - the "total" check that arithmetic uses to settle line items

  The 7 rows settled by arithmetic are a different case. There, the readers did 
  not agree (6.50 vs 8.50). Arithmetic picked the right one, and my hand check
  found 7 of 7 correct. It's still an ambiguity, and the brief asks us to flag
  ambiguous rows, so I'd keep them as a quick confirm rather than HIGH.

  What I'd do in the end:

  1. Fix the rounding bug in all three places. X51005444046 becomes HIGH. Then
     re-run and look for any other row where the readers agree and the sums pass
     but it's still flagged.
  2. Define the levels by the action needed:
     - HIGH: the readers agree and the arithmetic passes. Nothing to do.
     - MEDIUM, "Confirm": everything is filled, but the readers disagreed and
       arithmetic settled it. The page shows 8.50 (read 6.50 / 8.50), so it's a
       one-glance check.
     - LOW, "Fill in": a value or the line items are empty, or the sums fail.
       The reviewer reads the image and types it in.
  3. Rename the page labels to "Fill in" and "Confirm". Keep LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH in
     the CSV, since the brief asks for a confidence score you can sort by.

  Should I go ahead?

✻ Worked for 26s · done 3:51 PM

❯ I hope we are not overfitting to a case over here, and we are looking at the  
  wider range of scope. would this change the answer?                           

⏺ No, it doesn't change the answer. Neither change is built around one receipt.

  The rounding fix is a general rule. Every receipt follows items + adjustments
  + rounding (+ tax) = total. Our check left out two terms of that sum, so it
  was simply wrong for any receipt with rounding or a bill-level discount.
  Receipt X51005444046 just exposed it. The fix adds no thresholds and no
  special cases.

  Levels by action is also general. "Is anything empty?" and "did the readers
  disagree?" don't refer to any receipt, field or value.

  Where we could be overfitting:
  - All numbers are in-sample. We tuned and checked on the same 45 receipts, and
    the eval uses 15 labels from that same set. "0 wrong" and "7/7 picks
    correct" may look better than they would on new receipts.
  - Only one receipt format. They're all Malaysian GST receipts. Service charge,
    several tax rates, or items listed before a discount could break
    assumptions we haven't tested.

  How to stay honest:
  1. From here on, fix only bugs where a rule is plainly wrong, like the
     rounding one. No more tuning per receipt.
  2. Write the limits into NOTES.md: in-sample numbers, one format, and settle
     picks checked by hand on 7 cases only.
  3. For production, the next step is a held-out set: label new receipts we
     never tuned on, and measure there.

  So the plan stays the same: fix the rounding bug, set the levels by action,
  rename the labels, then stop tuning and document the limits. Should I go
  ahead?

✻ Cogitated for 14s · done 3:53 PM

❯ labels doesnt sound too  good, does it go with the UI?                        
  like would it be good ux?                                                     

⏺ No, I don't think they fit. Two problems:

  1. The page is read-only. "Fill in" suggests an input box, and there isn't
     one. The reviewer reads the image and fixes the value somewhere else (in
     the CSV). The label promises something the page doesn't do.
  2. It creates two vocabularies. The CSV says LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH and the page
     would say "Fill in/Confirm". A reviewer switching between them has to
     translate.

  Better: keep LOW / MEDIUM / HIGH everywhere, and explain each once.
  - Add a one-line legend under the filter buttons:
    LOW: a value is missing, read it from the image · MEDIUM: all filled, 
    confirm the highlighted value · HIGH: nothing to check
  - Each card already shows the action: "check" marks a missing value, and 8.50 
    (read 6.50 / 8.50) marks one to confirm. So the badge doesn't need to carry
    the action.

  One vocabulary, which matches the brief's "confidence score". The legend
  explains it once, and the cards show what to do.

  So the updated plan: fix the rounding bug, set the levels by action, add the
  legend, and document the limits. Should I go ahead?

✻ Crunched for 9s · done 3:54 PM

❯ okay and arithmetix will only pick when both reading have valies, if one says 
  illegible it stays low right?                                                 

⏺ Yes. Arithmetic only picks between two numbers.

  - Total: if one reader returns null (illegible or absent), pick() stops and
    nothing is chosen. The total stays empty and the row is LOW. A test covers
    this: "a total one reading left empty is never filled by arithmetic".
  - Line items: if either reader marks any item amount illegible, settling is
    skipped entirely. A reader with more amounts than the other can also never
    win, which is the made-up-items guard.

  One gap: a row with an illegible item amount is MEDIUM today. With levels by
  action, its line items stay empty, so it becomes LOW, which is what you'd
  expect.

  That existing test uses "absent", not "illegible". I'll add a test for an
  illegible total and one for an illegible item as part of this change. Going
  ahead now?

⏺ No, it doesn't make sense, and it's our bug. The line is printed as RM .02,
  with no leading zero. inQuote looks for the text 0.02, so it doesn't find .02
  and wrongly says the number isn't in its line. The wording "not found in
  printed line" also reads like "not printed", which makes it more confusing.

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

  so formats like .02, RM1,436.20, and (0.20) are handled correctly, and adjust
  the message wording accordingly.

  - Matching: the check now pulls every number out of the quoted line and

❯ [Image #24] here it says line items couldn't be reead, seems like model

  ⎿  [Image #24]

  Read 1 file, ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ These are set meals where sub-items have no printed price, so Flash's null
  values were correct while Haiku wrongly flagged them as illegible — the
  subtotal math (13.87+12.07=25.94) confirms no hidden amount exists. I'll add a
  general rule: if agreed-upon readable amounts already sum to the agreed
  subtotal, "illegible" lines can't be hiding a value and stay null, while
  genuinely cut-off receipts like X51005447844 will still fail the check.

⏺ Fixed. X51005337867 goes from LOW to HIGH, and it was the only row that
  changed. It now writes all 6 line items, with the 4 set sub-items left empty.

  It was a model mistake, not an unsupported format. Haiku marked the unpriced
  set items "illegible", against the prompt; Flash read them correctly.

  The rule (src/score.ts): when both readers agree on the readable amounts and
  those add up to an agreed subtotal or total, "illegible" item marks are
  ignored. No number is filled in.

  Two new tests: this receipt now scores HIGH, and a cut-off receipt whose
  readable amounts fall short of the total stays LOW. All 92 tests pass, and the
  eval still shows 0 wrong values in any status.

  Not done: a clearer prompt rule, such as "a line with only a tax code like S
  has no amount; it isn't illegible". It would change the prompt, so every
  receipt would be read again (about $0.87) and other readings could shift. The
  code rule already covers this case, so I'd leave the prompt alone.

  This fix and the RM .02 one aren't committed yet. Should I commit them?

✻ Cooked for 1m 6s · done 4:13 PM

❯ what do u mean code covers the case?                                          

⏺ I meant this: the prompt fix would stop Haiku from making the mistake at all.
  The code fix doesn't prevent it; it catches the mistake afterwards and ignores
  it, but only when arithmetic proves the "illegible" lines can't hold an
  amount.

  On this receipt, that proof works:
  - Both readers read the amounts as 13.87 and 12.07.
  - 13.87 + 12.07 = 25.94, which is the subtotal both readers agree on.
  - So the 4 "illegible" lines must have no amount, and the mark is ignored.

  "Covers" was too strong. The code catches it only when all of these hold:
  - both readers agree on the readable amounts
  - a subtotal or total is printed and both readers agree on it
  - the readable amounts add up to it exactly

  If any of these fails, for example the receipt has no subtotal and the total
  disagrees, or the readers also disagree on one real amount, the row stays LOW
  even though nothing is actually unreadable. That's a false alarm, not a wrong
  value, so it's the safe way to fail.

  So:
  - The code fix keeps the output correct whenever the arithmetic can prove it.
  - The prompt fix would reduce how often the mistake happens at all, including
    the cases the code can't prove. The cost is one full re-read (about $0.87),
    and every receipt's readings may shift, so we'd re-check the whole output.

  I'd keep only the code fix for now and list the prompt tweak in NOTES.md as
  the next step. If you'd rather do both, I can make the prompt change and
  re-run.

✻ Sautéed for 9s · done 4:16 PM

❯ [Image #25] this is confusing too                                             
  46 may not be printed and then we say "Total: 46")                            
  ⎿  [Image #25]

⏺ Two separate problems here.

  1. The wording contradicts itself. "46.00 may not be printed" next to the
     printed line Total : 46.00. The real question is whether that line is the
     subtotal. One reader said yes; the other said no subtotal is printed,
     because it's labelled "Total", not "Subtotal".
  2. Arithmetic can answer that question. 46.00 + 4.00 service + 3.00 GST =
     53.00, which is the total both readers agree on, and the items add up to
     46.00 too. So it is the subtotal, and this is the same definition noise as
     the "Total Amount" case before.

  The general fix: when one reader gives a subtotal and the other says none is
  printed, check that number against the values both agree on (total, tax,
  adjustments, rounding). If it fits, the readers agree. If not, it's still
  flagged, but the reason asks the real question: check subtotal: is "Total : 
  46.00" the subtotal?.

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Fixed. X51005337877 goes from LOW to HIGH, with subtotal 46.00 filled.

  What changed (src/score.ts):
  - Arithmetic check: when one reader gives a subtotal and the other says none
    is printed, the number is checked against the total, tax, adjustments and
    rounding both readers agree on. Here 46.00 + 4.00 + 3.00 = 53.00, so the
    readers agree.
  - Wording: when the number doesn't fit, the reason asks the real question:
    check subtotal: is "Total : 400.00" the subtotal? (400.00). "may not be
    printed" now only appears when no printed line was quoted. The same wording
    applies elsewhere, for example check adjustments: is "Discount 0.30" an 
    adjustment? (-0.30) on X51005442366.

  Tests: one for a subtotal that fits (HIGH, filled) and one that doesn't (LOW,
  empty, asks the question). All 93 pass. The eval still shows 0 wrong values;
  the counts are now LOW 8, MEDIUM 7, HIGH 29.

  Not committed yet: three fixes are waiting (the RM .02 match, the set items,
  and this one). Should I commit them as one commit?

❯ ok when sum doesnt match can we hve something in the UI like diff that this

⏺ Yes for the settled cases, with one change: I'd skip green and red.

  - Green says "correct", but these are MEDIUM rows that still ask the reviewer
    to confirm. It claims more certainty than we have.
  - Red and green are hard to tell apart for colour-blind reviewers, and red
    already means LOW on this page.

  Proposal, one style per case:

  ┌──────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────┐
  │       Case       │              Show               │      Example      │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Settled by       │ chosen value, and the rejected  │                   │
  │ arithmetic       │ reading struck through in grey  │ 8.50 6.50         │
  │ (MEDIUM)         │                                 │                   │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Not settled      │ both readings, no               │ check: 6.50 or    │
  │ (LOW)            │ strikethrough, since we don't   │ 8.50              │
  │                  │ know which is right             │                   │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Readers agree,   │ the sum written out, with the   │ 411.50 + 24.69 +  │
  │ sums fail (LOW)  │ mismatch marked                 │ 0.01 = 436.20 ≠   │
  │                  │                                 │ 440.00            │
  └──────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────┘

  The strikethrough tells the reviewer "we dropped this one", without claiming
  it's wrong. The written-out sum covers your literal case: the reviewer sees
  exactly which numbers don't add up, instead of reading a sentence.

  It's a small change, all in the review page, with tests. Should I build it?

✻ Worked for 42s · done 4:27 PM

❯ [Image #26] subtotal is there, it still says computed?                        
  ⎿  [Image #26]

⏺ You're right, the tag is misleading here. computed suggests no subtotal is
  printed, but Total Amount: $8.20 is right there.

  Why it happens:
  - On this receipt Total Amount (8.20) and Nett Total (8.20) are the same
    number, because GST is included in the prices.
  - Our normalization treats a "subtotal" equal to the total as the total
    printed twice and drops it. So 7.74 = 8.20 − 0.46 GST.
  - 7.74 is in fact the pre-tax amount, and it's also printed in the GST summary
    at the bottom. It's the useful value for the brief's check (subtotal + tax
    = total). The only thing wrong is the label.

  Recommendation: keep 7.74, and fix the tag to say what it is.
  - Tag: before tax instead of computed.
  - Tooltip with the formula: total 8.20 − tax 0.46. Rounding and adjustments
    are included when present.
  - CSV reason: subtotal before tax: total − tax instead of subtotal computed 
    (not printed).

  Why not show 8.20 instead: on tax-inclusive receipts, subtotal + tax would no
  longer equal the total, and the reviewer would see sums that don't add up. The
  same UNIHAKKA pattern appears on 5 receipts, so we'd trade one confusion for
  another.

  Should I make the label change?

✻ Churned for 22s · done 4:29 PM

❯ are we sure on this? you mean we'd show 7.74 computed, instead of 8.20?       

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ No, I'm not sure. I overstated it: this is a definition choice, not a fact. I
  checked all 10 receipts where a reader gave a subtotal equal to the total, and
  the data favours showing 8.20:

  - The line items add up to the printed number in every case. Here 7.10 + 1.10
    = 8.20. With 7.74, the brief's "line items sum to subtotal" check fails.
  - In 4 of the 10, both readers read it as the subtotal, e.g. X51005230621's
    printed Subtotal : 7.30. Our normalization still threw away a value both
    readers agreed on and put in a computed one. That's a real bug.
  - PLAN.md already says "Subtotal is copied exactly as printed (may include
    tax)", and the sums check already handles tax-inclusive receipts.

  On a tax-inclusive receipt, no single number passes both checks: 8.20 matches
  the line items, and 7.74 matches subtotal + tax = total. So we should show
  what's printed and make clear that it includes tax.

  Recommendation: remove the "subtotal equal to total → null" normalization. The
  subtotal rule we added later (the number must be in its quoted line and fit
  the agreed sums) already handles the noise it was meant to fix.
  - The card shows subtotal 8.20 with an incl. tax tag, so subtotal + tax ≠
    total doesn't look like an error.
  - Rows where both readers read it: printed value, still HIGH.
  - Rows where one reader read it:
    - X00016469671 and X51005230657 move from HIGH to MEDIUM. The readers really
      did disagree on whether that line is a subtotal, so MEDIUM is honest.
    - X51005442375 moves from MEDIUM to LOW. Haiku misread Total Amount: $8.50
      as 6.50, which doesn't fit the sums, so the subtotal is left for the
      reviewer.

  Should I make the change?

  What changed
  - Printed subtotal kept: I removed the "subtotal equal to total → drop it"
    normalization. The printed subtotal is kept, including the 4 receipts where
    both readers read it; we used to overwrite those with a computed value.
  - Same-line rule: a subtotal quoted from the same printed line as the total
    counts as the total, not a subtotal. I caught this in the diff: on
    X51005442388 Haiku quoted ***TOTAL RM 21.60 for both.
  - incl. tax tag: when the subtotal already includes tax, the card tags it, so
    subtotal + tax ≠ total reads as expected.

  Your card (X51005433543): subtotal 8.20 (read none / 8.20) with the incl. tax
  tag, from Total Amount: $8.20.

  - X00016469671 and X51005230657: HIGH → MEDIUM. Only one reader took the line
    as a subtotal, so it asks to confirm.
  - X51005442375: MEDIUM → LOW. Haiku misread Total Amount: $8.50 as 6.50, which
    doesn't fit the sums, so the subtotal is left to the reviewer.

✻ Crunched for 1m 24s · done 4:34 PM

❯ [Image #27] here seeems to be a problem as tax is already 0.45?               
  ⎿  [Image #27]

⏺ Yes, it's a problem, and it's the trade-off I pointed out last time. I picked
  the wrong side of it.

  The receipt:
  - The line items (1.80 + 1.91 + 3.71 + 0.53) add up to 7.95, including GST.
  - The GST summary prints the pre-tax amount, 7.50, and the tax, 0.45.
  - Total Amt Incl. GST : 7.95 is a total, not a subtotal.

  The real issue is what "subtotal" means. The brief defines it through the
  check "does subtotal plus tax equal total?", so a subtotal is the amount
  before tax. By that definition 7.95 is wrong and 7.50 is right. The same
  applies to 8.20 vs 7.74 on your earlier card: 7.74 was the right value, and
  only its label ("computed (not printed)") was wrong.

  Proposal: subtotal always means before tax.
  1. A printed line that already includes tax is converted to before-tax (total
     − tax). "Includes tax" is decided by arithmetic, not by label: tax > 0, and
     that line plus adjustments and rounding already equals the total. Here
     7.95 becomes 7.50.
  2. The tag says before tax, with a tooltip showing the source, e.g. total 7.95
     − tax 0.45. No more "not printed".
  3. A printed pre-tax subtotal is kept as printed, as before.
  4. Levels:
     - When one reader quoted a line that includes tax and the other found none,
       they don't disagree on the before-tax value. So this receipt becomes
       HIGH.
     - When one reader read a real pre-tax subtotal the other missed
       (X51005337877, 46.00), it stays MEDIUM, as you asked.

  Effect:
  - Receipts X51005230657, X51005433543 and X51005442334 lose the subtotal flag.
  - The rows where both readers read a tax-inclusive "Subtotal" show the pre-tax
    value, e.g. X51005230621 shows 6.89, which the GST summary also prints.
  - X51005442375 goes back to MEDIUM.
  - The same-line rule stays.

  This reverses my last change, so I'd rather confirm before building. Should I
  go ahead?

✻ Brewed for 39s · done 4:37 PM

❯ i feel this is prompt change, 7.5 is printed in gst summary there right? si   
  we defiine subtotal as before tax amount as printed                           
  if none printed, null,never computed                                          
  items can match subtotal or toal                                              
  right?                                                                        

⏺ Yes, that's cleaner, and it fits our "copy exactly as printed, never compute"
  rule. 7.50 is printed in the GST summary.

  New definition:
  - subtotal is the before-tax amount as printed: a "Subtotal" or "Excl. GST"
    line, or the GST summary's total amount. If none is printed, it's null,
    never computed.
  - Line items can match the subtotal or the total (minus adjustments and
    rounding).
  - Subtotal + tax + adjustments + rounding = total is checked only when a
    subtotal is printed.

  Two caveats:
  1. Several GST rates. The summary can have one row per rate (e.g. SR 6% and ZR
     0%). The prompt should say to use only a printed total row and never add
     up rows; otherwise we're computing again.
  2. Keep a safety net in code. A model may still pick Total Amount: $8.20. If
     the reported subtotal plus adjustments and rounding already equals the
     total while tax > 0, it includes tax, so the code sets it to null. This is
     decided by arithmetic, not by label text.

  What goes away: computed subtotals, the "computed" and "incl. tax" tags, and
  the "subtotal computed (not printed)" reason.

  Cost: the prompt changes, so all 44 receipts are read again. That's about
  $0.87 and a few minutes. Every reading can shift, so I'd re-run the eval and
  re-check the flagged cards afterwards.

  Should I go ahead, including the re-read?

  trust it.

⏺ Found three causes behind the HIGH drop: an ambiguous prompt for single-rate
  receipts, a quote-parser bug mishandling dot runs like ".6.00", and a reader
  computing a subtotal despite the quote. I'll fix the parser bug and the
  subtotal-computation issue in code first.

⏺ The new definition works, but my prompt wording is causing most of the new
  MEDIUMs. The eval still shows 0 wrong values in any status.

  Now: LOW 11, MEDIUM 15, HIGH 18 (before this change: LOW 9, MEDIUM 10, HIGH
  25).

  Fixed in code:
  - Dot runs in quotes: A......6.00 is now read as 6.00, not .6 and .00. There's
    a test for it.
  - Computed subtotals are dropped: a subtotal not in its own quoted line is set
    to null. X51005442382's 6.23 (6.60 − 0.37) is gone, and the row is back to
    HIGH.

  Still open:
  1. My prompt wording (10 of the 15 MEDIUMs are subtotals). "One row per rate
     and no total row → null" makes the readers split on a GST summary with a
     single rate row: one takes it (e.g. SR = GST @6% | 7.74), the other returns
     none. The code settles these correctly, but it's noise I created. The fix
     is one sentence: "a single-row tax summary: use its amount; several rows
     and no total row: null". That needs a second re-read (about $0.70).
  2. Real misreads after the re-read: vendors on 3 receipts (e.g. SHN vs SIN
     LIANHAP), and a day/month swap on X51005568890. All are caught, LOW, and
     left empty. That's the pipeline working, but it shows how much a new prompt
     shifts the readings.
  3. Haiku put a payment line into adjustments again (Mastercard -140.05,
     X51005568855), even though the prompt forbids it. It's caught as a disputed
     adjustment, and the row is MEDIUM.

  Recommendation: make the one-sentence prompt fix and re-read once more, then
  stop changing the prompt. Each re-read shifts the readings, so more prompt
  tuning on these 44 receipts would be overfitting.

  Nothing is committed. Should I make the prompt fix and re-read?

  ⎿  Updated src/extract.ts (+1 -1)
      18  - vendor: the business name printed on the same line as, or dir
          ectly above, the company registration number (e.g. "(123456-X)"
          , "Co. No."). This may be a legal name like "... Sdn Bhd" rathe
          r than the brand name at the top. If no registration number is 
          printed, the name at the top.
      19  - date: the transaction date as yyyy-mm-dd. Printed dates are d
          ay-first (dd/mm/yy or dd/mm/yyyy).
      20  - line_items: one entry per product. A discount printed under a
          n item is its own entry with a negative amount. An item with no
           printed price (e.g. part of a set) gets null unit_price and am
          ount. If an item's amount is printed but cut off or unreadable,
           set amount null and illegible true; never fill it from qty × u
          nit price.
      21 -- subtotal: the amount before tax, as printed. For example a "S
         -ubtotal", "Sub Total", "Total Sales (Excluding GST)" or "Total 
         -Excl. Tax" line, or the total row of the GST/tax summary (its a
         -mount column, not its tax column). Copy it as printed. Null if 
         -no before-tax amount is printed; never compute it. Never use a 
         -line that includes tax (e.g. "Total Amount" when prices include
         - GST, "Total Sales (Inclusive of GST)", "Total Amt Incl. GST"),
         - the final total, or a quantity line. If the tax summary has on
         -e row per rate and no total row, null: never add rows up.      
      21 +- subtotal: the amount before tax, as printed. For example a "S
         +ubtotal", "Sub Total", "Total Sales (Excluding GST)" or "Total 
         +Excl. Tax" line, or the total row of the GST/tax summary (its a
         +mount column, not its tax column). Copy it as printed. Null if 
         +no before-tax amount is printed; never compute it. Never use a 
         +line that includes tax (e.g. "Total Amount" when prices include
         + GST, "Total Sales (Inclusive of GST)", "Total Amt Incl. GST"),
         + the final total, or a quantity line. A tax summary with a sing
         +le row: use that row's amount. Several rows (one per rate) and 
         +no total row: null, never add rows up.                         
      22  - taxes: each tax amount printed (e.g. GST, SST). Include it ev
          en if the receipt says it is already included in the total.
      23  - adjustments: bill-level discounts (negative), service charge,
           tip. Not rounding. Not discounts already listed as line items.
           Not summary lines such as "Total Savings". Not payment lines (
          cash, card, change).
      24  - rounding: the printed rounding adjustment, signed. Null if no
          t printed.

⏺ This is where I'd stop changing the prompt, as agreed. The eval still shows 0
  wrong values, and vendor improved to 14 right / 1 left for review.

  Now: LOW 10, MEDIUM 14, HIGH 20.

  Why subtotal is still the most common MEDIUM (9 rows): it isn't the wording
  any more, it's how the two readers behave.
  - Flash leaves the subtotal empty more often (7 of 8 direct splits), even for
    lines the prompt names. It's conservative, and the other reader's printed
    value settles it.
  - In the other rows, one reader picked a line that includes tax (e.g. Subtotal
    7.30) and normalization dropped it, while the other reader got the pre-tax
    line (Total Sales Excluding GST : 6.89).
  - Either way the readers really did disagree, so under our rule MEDIUM is
    right. The cards show the quoted line, which makes each one a quick confirm.

  The trade-off with the new definition: HIGH went from 25 to 20. In return,
  every filled subtotal is now printed and before tax, and none is computed.
  That's the more honest output, and it's what the brief's subtotal + tax =
  total check needs.

  Other real flags in this run:
  - X51005337877 now shows two printed candidates, 46.00 and 50.00 (the GST
    summary). The service charge is taxed, so the two lines differ. It's LOW
    because two printed lines were read and neither was dropped.
  - X51005337867: one reader quoted Total: 30.24 for a total of 30.25, caught by
    the quote check.

  Not committed: the new prompt, the subtotal definition, the dot-run fix,
  dropping computed subtotals, test updates and the regenerated out/. Should I
  commit them, and add one line to NOTES.md on the subtotal definition ("before
  tax, as printed, never computed")?
